irregularities
All phenomenaSearchDraft

IOI circuit

A circuit helps GPT-2 select the appropriate name in indirect-object sentences. Wang et al., 2022 ↗

In brief

In GPT-2 small, a proposed circuit of attention heads helps the model predict the recipient's name in indirect-object sentences. Independent studies support several of its parts, but whether it fully explains the behavior is contested. Stricter tests find task-relevant information outside the circuit, and its evaluations depend on how parts are removed and what is measured. When the main name-copying heads are removed, other heads can compensate under some conditions, a finding others have reproduced. Some head roles recur in other models, but a shared complete circuit across models, training stages and prompt variants remains contested.

Papers to start with: Wang et al., 2022; Shi et al., 2024; Makou et al., 2026

Claims

SupportsLimitsChallenges
V3IOI head roles recur, but a shared circuit across models remains contested 16171 Suggested status CcontestedIndependent studies report recurring IOI head roles and reuse of circuit components on other tasks, but a shared complete circuit across models and training stages remains contested. Removing heads proposed to copy names into the output can improve IOI, and some heads that suppress signals in the mature model show little effect early in training.
Original resultWang et al., 2022 ↗

The original study preliminarily examined which GPT-2 medium heads directly influenced IOI logits.

A sparse set of heads directly influenced the logits. These heads did not all attend to the indirect object and subject.

Measurements and conditions (1)

Interpretability in the Wild: a Circuit for Indirect Object Identification in GPT-2 small

GPT-2 medium · Preliminary analysis of heads directly influencing IOI logits · Analysis: Preliminary

Direct influence on logits and attention to IO and S: A sparse set of heads directly influenced the logits, but not all of these heads attended to IO and S

Representative studies

Related findings4 groups of studies

Results that bear on this claim without testing it as worded.

Mechanisms correspond partially across models and languages 6 studies

Comparisons across models and languages find partial correspondence in importance, structural signatures, and suppression signals. Concentration and attended token roles still differ.

  • ILP-signature transfer identifies accepted IOI-matching candidates in Pythia-1B. N. Aljaafari et al., 2026 ↗
IOI mechanisms change during training 4 studies

Training changes attention patterns, component identities, and causal roles. Head identities can turn over despite stable performance, and early repetition processing can become helpful later.

  • Transplanting mature suppression-head parameters partly closes the early-to-mature IOI logit-difference gap. T. Dahiya et al., 2026 ↗
  • IOI head identities change during training while task performance remains stable. Curt Tigges et al., 2024 ↗
Post-training changes circuit contributions 4 studies

Fine-tuning and compression can change IOI roles and the concentration of causal effects. Structurally preserved heads can reverse their contributions after pruning.

  • Pruning structurally retains circuit heads but reverses contributions from name-copying heads and heads providing backup. Nabeelah Faumi et al., 2026 ↗
Constructed models reveal architectural requirements 2 studies

Constructed models implement simplified IOI roles, with success depending on architecture and positional information. In the symbolic version of IOI, which abstracts away natural-language details, two heads solve a task that a one-layer, one-head model using only attention cannot.

  • Two attention heads solve symbolic IOI where one head with the same parameter budget fails. Rabin Adhikari, 2025 ↗

Settings and limits

V4Different methods, ablations and metrics recover essentially the same IOI circuit 14226 Suggested status CcontestedMethod independence is contested despite several independent groups recovering overlapping circuit components. Recovered circuits vary with discovery method, ablation, metric, random seed and traversal choices.

  • LimitsReplacing parts of MLP8 activations contradicted the circuit; removing a disconnected component weakened these effects. Aleksandar Makelov et al., 2023 ↗
  • ChallengesArbitrary discovery changes produced IOI edge circuits with low overlap. Xi Chen et al., 2026 ↗

Appendix F.3

Swapping parts of activations implicated excluded MLP8 and even a random MLP, unlike whole-component swapping. Georg Lange, 2023 ↗

An Interpretability Illusion for Activation Patching of Arbitrary Subspaces

DAS subspace patching on MLP components (MLP8, or a random MLP at that position)

Subspace patching implicated MLP8 with logit difference about −1.3, whereas whole-component patching gave +0.4; a random MLP at the same position produced a similar illusion.

Source check disagrees: it reads this result as a challenge.

Cited measurements (3)

gpt2-small · Patch full MLP8 activations between prompts with opposite position information. · Location: MLP8, END token; Prompts: ABB and BAB patterns; Patch: full activations

Change in logit difference between the correct and incorrect name: increases by ~0.4

Finding a position direction in MLP8

gpt2-small · Patch the 1-dimensional DAS direction in MLP8 between prompts with opposite position information. · Location: MLP8, END token; Prompts: ABB and BAB patterns; Patch: DAS-learned 1-dimensional direction

Change in logit difference between the correct and incorrect name: decreases by ~1.3; described as a ~3x stronger effect in the opposite direction (baseline: Full MLP8 patch increases logit difference by ~0.4)

Finding a position direction in MLP8

gpt2-small · Train a DAS subspace on randomly sampled MLP weights and biases, then add the patched-versus-unpatched random-MLP output difference to the real MLP8 output. · Location: MLP8 position in the network; Random weights: norm of output activations matched to MLP8; Intervention: random MLP is not substituted for MLP8

Change in logit difference: reduces logit difference even more than the MLP8 direction (baseline: MLP8 DAS direction)

DAS subspaces exist in MLPs with random weights

Attribution Patching: Activation Patching At Industrial Scale

Residual-stream and MLP0 patches, and the non-counterfactual attention attribution

Some Lessons Learned from Studying Indirect Object Identification in GPT-2 small

Related findings3 groups of studies

Results that bear on this claim without testing it as worded.

Discovery choices change circuit quality 17 studies

Discovered IOI circuits differ in size and in how closely they reproduce the model’s behaviour, depending on attribution methods, subgraph selection procedures, numerical precision, and other implementation choices.

  • At matched sparsity, Edge Pruning has lower IOI distribution error (KL divergence) than ACDC and EAP. Adithya Bhaskar et al., 2024 ↗
Attribution accuracy varies across components 4 studies

Agreement between attribution approximations and activation patching depends on the component, patch size, position, and model. Approximation errors can change both estimated effects and component rankings.

  • First-order attribution patching underestimates an attention head’s effect, ranking it lower than activation patching does. Lu-Yang Zhang et al., 2026 ↗
Circuit stability depends on sampling and representation 4 studies

Discovered circuit structure varies under prompt resampling. Stability depends on the scoring method, representation, and model.

  • Nonoverlapping prompt groups yield similar IOI-specific circuits in larger tested models, but not Pythia-14M. N. Aljaafari et al., 2026 ↗
V5IOI circuit evaluations depend on ablation, metric and prompt distribution 2531 Suggested status R2independently reproducedSeveral independent groups find that circuit evaluations change with evaluation choices, with evidence extending to Llama and Qwen models. The circuit explains only part of the behavior, although some evaluation changes leave results stable.
Original resultWang et al., 2022 ↗

The authors evaluated the GPT-2 small attention-head circuit using ablations and searches over removed nodes.

The circuit retained 87% of the full model’s mean logit difference—the average output-score margin for the recipient’s name over the subject’s—a gap of 0.46. Differences between circuit and full-model responses to removals, measured by incompleteness scores, were small for random removals and removals of whole head classes; greedy searches for removals found differences up to 3.09, compared with the original logit difference of 3.56.

Section 4.3.1; Figure 2

Related findings1 group of studies

Results that bear on this claim without testing it as worded.

Evaluation choices change conclusions 10 studies

Circuit evaluations depend on how interventions are performed, how reference values are defined, and which metrics are measured. Related comparisons outside IOI show that these choices can also reverse method rankings.

  • How much of an attention head’s effect is explained depends on the evaluation metric. Callum McDougall et al., 2023 ↗

How it works

V1GPT-2 small’s 26-head, seven-class IOI circuit is faithful, complete and minimal 31171 Suggested status CcontestedIn indirect object identification (IOI), the model predicts the recipient’s name; independent studies support several circuit components, but contest whether the proposed circuit fully explains this behavior. Stricter tests find connections that can be removed and task-relevant information outside the circuit; how well the circuit reproduces model behavior depends on evaluation methods, and one pathway’s effect changes direction.
Original resultWang et al., 2022 ↗

Wang et al. studied IOI in GPT-2 small using path patching (replacing signals along chosen connections) and attention-head ablations (removing head contributions).

The proposed circuit retained 87% of the full model’s mean logit difference—the average output-score margin for the recipient’s name over the subject’s name—and sampled removals produced small discrepancies between circuit and full-model responses to those removals. In the minimality tests, removing each of its 26 heads changed the original logit difference by at least 1%.

Section 5.1

Related findings8 groups of studies

Results that bear on this claim without testing it as worded.

Head functions extend beyond IOI 10 studies

IOI-associated heads also support broader copying, inhibition, and language-model functions. Unclear membership of one pooled group of removed heads limits attribution to particular head classes.

  • Repeat versus no-repeat ablation effects vary by convention, with pooled head membership unclear. P. Balogh, 2026 ↗
Head roles separate into finer computations 9 studies

IOI head roles can separate into distinct features, subspaces, directions, and attention channels. These finer representations can distinguish subject position from subject identity.

  • Feature-level distributed alignment search reduces mean squared errors for subject position and identity versus full-vector search. Aaron Mueller et al., 2025 ↗
Intervention signals concentrate in later layers 6 studies

IOI intervention signals often concentrate in middle-to-late attention layers and final-token sites. Recovery depends on the selected components, corruption, and patching procedure.

  • Under BABA corruption, whole-layer patching restores more IOI logit difference at progressively later tested layers. Hussein Chouman et al., 2026 ↗
  • Patching selected heads restores about 96–97% of the clipped IOI logit-difference gap. A. Asiaee, 2026 ↗
Circuit criteria do not identify a unique mechanism 5 studies

Faithfulness measures how well a circuit reproduces model behaviour, overlap measures shared components, completeness tests for missing contributions outside the circuit, and minimality tests whether any component is unnecessary. Under specified assumptions, toy examples show that multiple subcircuits can meet these criteria; this does not establish an observed IOI mechanism.

  • Under specified assumptions, distinct toy subcircuits can match behaviour, omit no contributions, and exclude unnecessary components. Iván Arcuschin, 2024 ↗
Representation choices change apparent circuit size 4 studies

Separating token positions and choosing nodes, neurons, features, or edges changes apparent circuit compactness and reuse. The reported compact CD-T circuit is not assigned perfect faithfulness.

  • A position-specific circuit uses 0.4% of model components, without showing that it perfectly reproduces model behaviour. Aliyah R. Hsu et al., 2024 ↗
MLPs carry context and competing predictions 3 studies

MLPs contribute contextual and distributional computations beyond the attention-head account. Some learned associations compete with the indirect-object answer.

  • Removing later MLP computation suppresses competing predictions and raises Pythia-1B’s indirect-object probability relative to 'the'. Lei Chen et al., 2024 ↗
Weight geometry partly reflects circuit function 3 studies

Weight subspaces, component alignments, and weight swaps recover parts of IOI head classes and pathways. Correspondence remains incomplete and varies across models.

  • Differences from swapping weights separate expert-defined IOI head groups better than random groupings in GPT-2 small. Tal Haklay et al., 2026 ↗
Different edge circuits can sustain similar performance 2 studies

Structurally distinct edge subgraphs can sustain similar IOI performance. Behavioural sufficiency therefore does not identify a unique circuit.

  • DiscoGP circuit search finds IOI circuits sharing few components, averaging about 80% accuracy in Pythia-160M. Xi Chen et al., 2026 ↗
V2Backup heads compensate when main heads that copy names are ablated 850 Suggested status R2independently reproducedFollow-up studies support compensation by other heads under some conditions after the main name-copying heads are ablated in GPT-2 small. Larger models remain unassessed.

The authors ablated all Name Mover Heads (heads that copy names into the output) in GPT-2 small and used path patching to test other heads’ effects on output scores.

The mean IOI logit difference dropped by 5%, and the task still worked. Eight selected Backup Name Mover Heads compensated for the loss of the main heads.

Self Repair

Challenges0 sources

No sources in this group.

Related findings3 groups of studies

Results that bear on this claim without testing it as worded.

Component effects depend on their interactions 6 studies

Joint interventions reveal redundancy and interactions that single-component effects miss. Including pairwise interactions also improves predictions of the effects of removing subsets of components, without establishing a particular backup mechanism.

  • Setting both a circuit’s first attention head and MLP to zero reveals interactions between them. Abdallah Khemais, 2026 ↗
  • Pairwise interactions improve held-out predictions of GPT-2 small’s responses to removing subsets of components. V. Erramilli, 2026 ↗
Compensation extends beyond name-copying heads 5 studies

After components are removed, other computations can offset the loss at the layer level and within individual heads, beyond IOI heads that copy names to the output. Factual-recall results show compensation by later attention computations and less cancellation of signals by MLPs; broader findings depend on normalization (rescaling) and training.

  • Later components restore about 70% of ablation-induced output-score loss in Chinchilla 7B factual recall. Tom McGrath et al., 2023 ↗
Conditional interventions reveal backup activity 3 studies

Backup activity depends on signals from primary components. Query coupling and interventions after primary removal reveal repair that intact-state attribution can miss.

  • Backup heads’ repair mainly uses name-copying output that does not directly affect the indirect-object name’s score. Callum McDougall et al., 2023 ↗
V6The same IOI circuit operates across name orders, templates and additional names 1081 Suggested status CcontestedThe claim that one circuit handles all prompt variants remains contested. Independent studies support reuse of some circuit components across prompt versions in GPT-2 small, but transfer between versions can fail; these failures challenge broader reuse without directly testing the original circuit of attention heads.
Original resultWang et al., 2022 ↗

Wang et al. tested GPT-2 small's task performance after adding a subject or indirect-object mention, without testing circuit reuse.

An extra subject mention gave a logit difference of 3.64 versus 3.55 at baseline. An extra indirect-object mention reduced it to 1.23, with the subject logit exceeding the indirect-object logit in 23.4% of cases versus 0.7% at baseline.

Related findings3 groups of studies

Results that bear on this claim without testing it as worded.

Wording and format affect IOI performance 7 studies

IOI performance changes with lexical content, repetition, templates, and tokenization. Some discovered edge circuits nevertheless preserve accuracy under added distractors.

  • GPT-2 small fails far more often when IOI prompts describe transferring 'the keys' between people. M. Naser-Moghadasi, 2026 ↗
  • GPT-2 small logit differences fall from base IOI through DoubleIO to TripleIO. Jatin Nainani et al., 2024 ↗
Individual prompts use partly different circuits 5 studies

Component importance and intervention recovery vary across IOI prompts. Shared components coexist with substantial prompt-specific variation that dataset averages can obscure.

  • A selected six-head GPT-2 small subset’s coverage of IOI behaviour varies substantially across individual prompts. Ajay Pravin Mahale, 2026 ↗
  • Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct prompts with different names yield circuits sharing some heads but differing at many sites. Abdallah Khemais, 2026 ↗
Positional signals depend on prompt structure 3 studies

GPT-2 small encodes position and name order in ways that can be decoded. Its IOI positional signals also depend on structure relative to conjunctions and on prompt prefixes.

  • Keeping duplicate-name position relative to 'and' restores more output-score difference than replacing 'and' with 'alongside'. Connor Kissane, 2024 ↗

Validating causal representations

Features describe activation patterns, probes test what information activations contain, and alignments map activations to proposed computations. Test these explanations by intervening on model computations, checking what the explanation method can fit, and checking whether it works on new examples.

Selected features need not exhaust causal representations 3 studies

Sparse autoencoder (SAE) features related to IOI can correlate with behaviour without capturing its entire causal representation. Alternative directions, redundant representations, and sensitivity to the random seed limit claims that IOI behaviour must pass through these features.

  • Changes to activations passed between layers restore IOI decisions while sparse-autoencoder features stay fixed. Mingyu Cui et al., 2026 ↗
Alignment success needs stronger controls 3 studies

Accurately mapping activations to proposed computations or decoding information from activations does not by itself show that a model implements an IOI algorithm. Test separately what mappings the alignment method can fit and whether it works on prompts or entities absent from the examples used to fit it.

  • Nonlinear search perfectly maps activations to IOI computations in randomly initialized Pythia, which cannot perform IOI. De Sutter et al., 2025 ↗

Where to start reading

  1. 1

    Kevin Wang et al., 2022 ↗ Interpretability in the Wild: a Circuit for Indirect Object Identification in GPT-2 small

    Introduces GPT-2 small’s circuit, tests when each head is necessary, and shows backup responses after name-copying heads are removed.

  2. 2

    Claudia Shi et al., 2024 ↗ Hypothesis Testing the Circuit Hypothesis in LLMs

    Independently tests the circuit against random references and stricter criteria, exposing dependence on ablation choices.

  3. 3

    Alireza Bayat Makou et al., 2026 ↗ MURANO: Design, Run, and Reproduce Mechanistic Interpretability Experiments as Composable Pipelines

    Reproduces selected signal routes with code and comparison tests; name-copying effects depend on the intervention; whole-circuit validation remains open.

Known gaps in this draft

Open issues concern missing supporting evidence, interpretation of circuit comparisons, reading-list coverage, independent corroboration, and completeness of the available evidence.

All 6 gaps
  • The discussion treats a comparison of separately discovered circuits as evidence that evaluations can be stable: one was found using mean ablation (replacing signals with their averages), the other using zero ablation (setting signals to zero). This comparison does not test whether changing the ablation method changes the evaluation of the same circuit.
  • The supporting evidence omits the configuration with complete agreement against a median discovered circuit. This agreement does not establish recovery of the original GPT-2 circuit.
  • The supporting evidence omits conditions under which discovered circuits transferred successfully in both directions. These circuits specify connections between components, and their transfer does not establish that the original heads’ computations were preserved.
  • The reading list omits direct evidence that the same heads can process features differently when names appear in a different order. Sharing heads does not by itself show that the computation is preserved.
  • It remains unassessed whether independent studies confirm that different circuit structures can explain the behavior and that components enhance one another’s effects when combined.
  • Unreported source availability and extraction limits prevent assessing whether the evidence coverage is complete.
